Thus,
Kv_{q}(\zeta_{q^{\infty}})
must be
q
-closed (recall that the maximal pro-
q
Galois extension of
Kv_{q}
has Galois group
\mathbb{Z}_{q}
).

From
Kv_{q}(\zeta_{q^{\infty}})
being
q
-closed and
\charK Kv_{K}=q
, it follows that the residue field of
Kv_{q}(\zeta_{q^{\infty}})
with respect to the unique prolongation of
\overline{v_{K}}
is equal to
Kv_{K}
and must be
q
-closed as well. In positive characteristic
q
, cyclotomic extensions by
\zeta_{q^{q^{n}}-1}
have degree dividing
q^{n}
. It follows that
\zeta_{q^{q^{n}}-1}\in Kv_{K}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
However, note that we have

\gcd\bigl(q^{q^{n+1}}-1,\,q^{q^{n}}-1\bigr)=q^{q^{n}}-1,

which is an instance of the well-known identity
\gcd(a^{k}-1,a^{l}-1)=a^{\gcd(k,l)}-1
. In conjunction with the above, this implies that there are infinitely many primes
q^{\prime}
with
\zeta_{q^{\prime}}\in Kv_{K}
.
This contradicts
Step 1.3.
, where we showed that
Kv_{K}
contains a
q^{\prime}
-th primitive root of unity for primes
q^{\prime}\neq p,q
if and only if
q^{\prime}\mid(p-1)
. We conclude
\charK Kv_{K}\neq q
for all primes
q\neq p
.

Step 1.5.

To exclude the remaining case, assume that
\charK Kv_{K}=0
. Here, we will eventually arrive at a contradiction by considering the ramification data for explicit extensions of
(K,v_{K})
implied by this assumption.
10
10

10

For this purpose, Pop uses a different type of argument. In the proof of
[
44
, (1.4)]
, he shows that the Sylow
p
-subgroups of any non-trivial normal subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
are not pro-cyclic. However, if
\charK Kv_{K}=0
, then considering the action in the semi-direct product of Theorem
2.27
shows that
G_{K}
has a normal pro-cyclic subgroup of the form
\prod_{r_{q}\neq 1}\mathbb{Z}_{q}
, which has a pro-cyclic Sylow
p
-subgroup.

1) We first show that
v_{K}K
is
p
-divisible. Assume towards a contradiction that

r_{p}\coloneqq\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(v_{K}K/pv_{K}K)\geq 1,

and let
K_{p}
be the fixed field of some Sylow
p
-subgroup of
G_{K}
.
By ramification theory (Facts
2.21
,
2.22
,
2.27
), the absolute ramification subgroup of
K_{p}
with respect to the unique prolongation of
v_{K}
to
K^{\sepc}
is trivial, so
I_{K_{p}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{r_{p}}
is a non-trivial closed normal abelian subgroup of
G_{K_{p}}
. Moreover,
G_{K_{p}}\not\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\rtimes C_{2}
; for instance,
K_{p}
admits
C_{p}^{3}
-extensions by the Degree Lemma
3.15
.
Hence, the absolute Galois groups
G_{K}\cong G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
satisfy the hypotheses of the Galois characterisation of henselianity (Theorem
3.29
).
In particular, both
K
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
must admit a henselian valuation tamely branching at
p
.
However, as we have seen in Example
2.52
, the only henselian valuations on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
are the trivial valuation and the standard
p
-adic valuation (which has residue characteristic
p
), neither of which is tamely branching at
p
. Therefore,
v_{K}K
is
p
-divisible.

2) Next, we claim that
K
admits a unique
C_{p-1}\times C_{p-1}
-extension
L
. The existence follows from the fact that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
admits a
C_{p-1}\times C_{p-1}
-extension, namely
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{(p-1)^{2}},\zeta_{p})/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. To show uniqueness, observe that any such extension is the compositum of its
C_{q^{r}}\times C_{q^{r}}
-subextensions, where
q^{r}
ranges over the prime powers in the factorisation of
p-1
. At the same time, each
C_{q^{r}}\times C_{q^{r}}
-extension of
K
is unique, for otherwise, the Galois group of the compositum of two distinct
C_{q^{r}}\times C_{q^{r}}
-extensions would have
q
-rank 3. For primes
q\mid(p-1)
this is incompatible with
G_{K_{q}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{q}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}
having
q
-rank 2.

3) We claim that
K
admits a unique
C_{p-1}
-extension unramified at
v_{K}
, which can moreover be characterised uniquely in Galois-theoretic terms.
From
Step 1.3.
, we know that

\Gal(Kv_{K}(q)/Kv_{K})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{q}.

In particular,
Kv_{K}
has a unique
C_{q^{r}}
-extension for each prime power
q^{r}|(p-1)
. Arguing again via composita, we infer that
Kv_{K}
admits a unique
C_{p-1}
-extension that induces a unique unramified
C_{p-1}
-extension
L_{1}/K
. Using our description of the prime-to-
p
roots in
Step 1.3.
and the proof of Lemma
3.6
, one can explicitly write
L_{1}=K(\zeta_{(p-1)^{2}})
. By Lemma
3.4
and Remark
3.5
, this gives rise to a Galois-theoretic description of
L_{1}
: it is the unique
C_{p-1}
-extension that itself admits a
C_{q^{2r}}\times C_{q^{2r}}
-extension for every prime power
q^{r}\mid(p-1)
.
Moreover, we can identify totally ramified extensions: any
C_{p-1}
-extension of
K
that is linearly disjoint from
L_{1}
over
K
must be totally ramified by the uniqueness of
L_{1}
.

4.1) We now derive the contradiction for
p\neq 2
. Consider the
C_{p}
-extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p})
obtained by adjoining
\sqrt[p]{p}
. Over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, it has a non-abelian Galois group

\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p},\!\sqrt[p]{p})/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cong C_{p}\rtimes C_{p-1}.

Being totally ramified, it is linearly disjoint from
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{(p-1)^{2}})
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Translating over to
K
, we find a Galois extension
L_{3}/K
with Galois group
C_{p}\rtimes C_{p-1}
and
C_{p-1}
-subextension
L_{2}/K
, such that
L_{1}
and
L_{3}
are linearly disjoint over
K
(with
L_{1}
uniquely determined by the above Galois property).
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{p-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{p-1}}

As noted before,
L_{2}/K
is totally ramified. Since
v_{K}K
is
p
-divisible, the
C_{p}
-extension
L_{3}/L_{2}
must be unramified. But then,
L_{3}
is the compositum of
L_{2}
and the inertia subfield of
L_{3}/K
. Therefore,
\Gal(L_{3}/K)\cong C_{p-1}\times C_{p}
is abelian, contrary to our assumption.

4.2) Finally, consider the case
p=2
. We need to modify the above constructions slightly to work around the triviality of the
C_{p-1}\times C_{p-1}
-extension. Let us instead start with the
C_{2}
-extension
\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3})/\mathbb{Q}_{2}
. It admits a unique
C_{3}\times C_{3}
-extension given by
\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{9},\!\sqrt[3]{2})/\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3})
, where
\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{9})/\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3})
is the unique unramified
C_{3}
-extension and
\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3},\!\sqrt[3]{2})/\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3})
is a totally ramified
C_{3}
-extension. By Kummer Theory and the Degree Lemma
3.15
, the field
\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3},\!\sqrt[3]{2})
admits a
C_{2}^{8}
-extension.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\zeta_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{2}^{8}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{2}}

Let
L_{1}\coloneqq K(\zeta_{9})
be the unramified
C_{3}
-extension of
K(\zeta_{3})
, uniquely identified as the only one that admits a
C_{9}\times C_{9}
-extension. Furthermore, let
L_{2}/K(\zeta_{3})
be a
C_{3}
-extension such that
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are linearly disjoint over
K(\zeta_{3})
,
L_{3}/K(\zeta_{3})
is Galois, and
L_{3}/L_{2}
has Galois group
C_{2}^{8}
. As before,
L_{2}/K(\zeta_{3})
is totally ramified and
L_{3}/L_{2}
is unramified by the
2
-divisibility of
v_{K}K
. Therefore, the inertia subfield of
L_{3}/K(\zeta_{3})
is a
C_{2}^{8}
-extension of
K(\zeta_{3})
. This is, however, impossible: the degree of the maximal abelian Galois extension of exponent 2 of
K(\zeta_{3})
and
\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3})
must coincide. This degree is equal to
16
, as can be seen by Kummer theory and the Degree Lemma
3.15
,

\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{2}}\left(K(\zeta_{3})^{\times}/K(\zeta_{3})^{\times 2}\right)=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{2}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3})^{\times}/\mathbb{Q}_{2}(\zeta_{3})^{\times 2}\right)=4.

This concludes our proof that
\charK Kv_{K}=p
.

Step 2.

As now
(\charK K,\charK Kv_{K})=(0,p)
, we may consider the Standard Decomposition
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{K}:K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Kv_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Kv_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Kv_{K}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{v_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathrlap{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\overline{v}$}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\overline{v}$}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}}\phantom{v}}\lx@text@overline{\mathrlap{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\displaystyle\overline{v}$}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\textstyle\overline{v}$}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}}\phantom{v}}}

Each place in the decomposition is henselian (Fact
2.45
); by Remark
2.47
, the valuation
\overline{v_{p}}
is of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
and rank 1.

Step 2.1.

We first show that
G_{Kv_{0}}
cannot be realised as the absolute Galois group of a field of characteristic
p
. To rule this out, we argue along the same lines as in
Step 1.2.
.
By Lemma
3.27
, there exists a
C_{p-1}
-extension
M_{1}
of
K
with the property that it admits a
C_{p}
-extension
M_{2}
that does not embed into any
C_{p^{2}}
-extension of
M_{1}
. Let
v^{\prime}_{0}
and
v^{\prime\prime}_{0}
denote the unique prolongations of
v_{0}
to
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
, respectively. Since
\charK Kv_{0}=0
, the value group
v_{0}K
is
p
-divisible by the same argument as in the first part of
Step 1.5.
. In particular,
v^{\prime}_{0}M_{1}
is
p
-divisible, so
M_{2}/M_{1}
must be unramified and
M_{2}v^{\prime\prime}_{0}
is a
C_{p}
-extension of
M_{1}v^{\prime}_{0}
that does not embed into any
C_{p^{2}}
-extension, as we could lift any such embedding to
M_{1}
by henselianity.
This is a property of the absolute Galois group
G_{M_{1}v^{\prime}_{0}}
; by Lemma
3.25
, this profinite group cannot be realised in characteristic
p
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{2}v^{\prime\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{1}v^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Kv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{\prime\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{p-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{0}}

At the same time, note that the absolute Galois group
G_{K}=D_{K}
of
K
projects onto the absolute Galois group
G_{Kv_{0}}
of the residue field; in particular,
G_{K}
is small implies that
G_{Kv_{0}}
is small as well.
Assume that
\overline{v_{p}}(Kv_{0})
is
p
-divisible. By the Transfer Lemma
4.10
, the profinite group
G_{Kv_{0}}
can be realised as the absolute Galois group of a field of positive characteristic
p
. The same is true for the finite index open subgroup
G_{M_{1}v^{\prime}_{0}}
. We arrive at a contradiction, proving that
\overline{v_{p}}(Kv_{0})
is not
p
-divisible.

Step 2.2.

The smallness of
G_{Kv_{0}}
implies that
(Kv_{0})^{\times}/(Kv_{0})^{\times p}
is finite (Lemma
3.19
). By Pop’s Lemma
3.20
,
\overline{v_{p}}(Kv_{0})
is either discrete or
p
-divisible. We have shown the latter to be impossible. By the last part of Pop’s Lemma,
Kv_{p}
is finite.

Step 3.

As finite fields only admit trivial valuations, it follows that
\overline{\mathrlap{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{v}$}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\textstyle\overline{v}$}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}}\phantom{v}}\lx@text@overline{\mathrlap{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{v}$}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\textstyle\overline{v}$}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\overline{v}$}\kern-0.5pt}}\phantom{v}}
on
Kv_{p}
is trivial and
Kv_{p}=Kv_{K}
. In particular, the rank 1 valuation
\overline{v_{p}}
is the same as the valuation
\overline{v_{K}}
on
Kv_{0}
induced by
v_{K}
.

Step 3.1.

Using the finiteness of
Kv_{K}
, we can now identify the residue field
Kv_{K}
as
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. From
Step 1.3.
, we know that the prime-to-
p
roots of unity of
K
are
\mu_{p-1}
. This implies
Kv_{K}=\mathbb{F}_{p}
by Hensel’s Lemma.

Step 3.2.

To prove
v_{K}K\equiv\mathbb{Z}
, it suffices to show that
v_{K}K
has a convex subgroup
\Delta\cong\mathbb{Z}
such that
v_{K}K/\Delta
is divisible by Fact
5.2
. By
Step 2.2.
, the convex subgroup
\Delta\coloneqq\overline{v_{K}}(Kv_{0})
is a discrete. It remains to verify that
v_{K}K/\Delta\cong v_{0}K
is divisible.

We have already shown that
v_{0}K
is
p
-divisible in
Step 2.1.
. We established
(v_{K}K:qv_{K}K)=q
in
Step 1.1.
for all primes
q\neq p
, so the minimal positive element of
\Delta
already generates
v_{K}K/qv_{K}K
. Therefore,
v_{0}K
is divisible.

Step 3.3.

Let
L
again denote the unique
C_{p-1}\times C_{p-1}
-extension of
K
. The extension
K(\zeta_{p})/K
is abelian of degree dividing
p-1
, so it is contained in
L
, i.e.,
\zeta_{p}\in L
.
Write
v_{L}
for the unique extension of
v_{K}
to
L
, and assume
v_{K}
and
v_{L}
are normalised such that the minimal positive elements are both
1\in\mathbb{Z}
. It follows from our analysis in
Step 1.5.
that
L/K
has ramification index
e(L/K)=p-1
and degree of inertia
f\coloneqq f(L/K)=p-1
. Therefore, the “absolute ramification index” of
L
is given by
v_{L}(p)=(p-1)v_{K}(p)
.

Additionally, write
F\coloneqq\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{(p-1)^{2}},\zeta_{p})
for the unique
C_{p-1}\times C_{p-1}
-extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. By the Degree Lemma
3.15
and Remark
3.16
, we obtain

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\left(F^{\times}/F^{\times p}\right)

\displaystyle=(p-1)^{2}+2

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\left(L^{\times}/L^{\times p}\right)

\displaystyle=(p-1)^{2}v_{K}(p)+2.

Kummer theory says that these two quantities express the dimension of the maximal abelian Galois extension of exponent
p
of the unique
C_{p-1}\times C_{p-1}
-extensions of
F
and
L
.
These dimensions are determined by the isomorphic absolute Galois groups
G_{K}
and
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Since they must therefore coincide, it follows that
v_{K}(p)=1
is minimal positive, as desired.

34
